(6) whether the company's record of meeting its through-cycle targets (at least 10% sales growth, at least 14% EBITA margin, at least 20% return on capital employed) in past recoveries supports expecting the sales-growth and return targets to be met again, given that sales grew 2.2% in 2024 and fell 1.0% in 2025 and return on capital employed was 18% at June 2026; Layer 3 and Layer 4

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months
and Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

10 questions in 4 groups · 15,348 words · written 23 October 2026, 10:50 to 11:06 UK, Fable, ricky login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes for this layer

The Investment Analyst's title
Indutrade (INDT-SE): third and fourth sets of questions

Investment Analyst, written 2026-10-23. Third set: the predictability of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months (100 most positive for predictability and so for an investment case, 1 most negative, 50 neutral). Fourth set: how far our assessment is already reflected in guidance, sell-side estimates and the narrative (100 the most favourable gap for an investment case, 1 the least, 50 neither).

Reader's note: what the files do and do not contain today, and the corrections I carry into these answers

  1. The Q3 2026 report is published today, 23 October 2026, and its headline figures are not in my files. The AlphaSense report commissioned on it (OWN-1) states that the interim report's net sales, organic growth, order intake, EBITA by area, SG&A and management commentary were not retrievable on the day, and that no post-report broker note was indexed. Five lines of the report did reach the files through other reports and I use them: operating cash flow of SEK 1,216m in Q3 (Q2 2026: 882m) and a rolling twelve-month operating cash flow of SEK 4,331m; interest-bearing net debt of SEK 9,574m at 30 September 2026, down sequentially from SEK 9,821m at June (company basis) and 1.6x EBITDA; net financial items of SEK -331m for the nine months (nine months 2025: -334m); lease repayments of SEK 446m for the nine months (410m); and contingent consideration of SEK 959m carried (696m at December 2025), with SEK 387m recognised on the nine-month 2026 acquisitions, SEK 228m paid in the nine months for earlier years' deals, and an aggregate preliminary purchase price of SEK 2,508m for SEK 2,060m of acquired annual sales (1.22x sales). Everything on the income statement above the financial line remains the pre-print consensus: DNB Carnegie's preview of Q3 sales SEK 8,896m, adjusted EBITA 1,348m (15.1%) and organic growth 5.7%, against an Infront consensus of 8,827m, 1,342m and 15.2%, and against the second set's own quarterly input of revenue +11% (about SEK 8,709m), EBITA about 1,325m and 15.2%. Where an answer depends on the Q3 print, I say what each outcome would mean rather than guess the outcome.
  2. The peer prints of the last ten days are in the files and they matter. OEM International (15 October): organic sales +11%, orders +24%, record 16.5% EBITA margin. Addtech (21 October): organic +1% against hard comparisons, EBITA +14 to 17%, record 16% margin, acquisitions adding 5%, customers starting to rebuild inventories. Beijer Ref (22 October): organic +8%, sales +20%, FX +2%. Bufab (23 October): organic +6.5%, record 15.6% margin. Lifco (23 October): organic +1.6%, acquired +5.6%, margin 23.1%, FX +1.1%, and a warning that a customer worth 5% of group sales moves purchasing outside Europe from January 2027. Lagercrantz (23 October, as the OWN-1 report records it): revenue +22% and organic +12%. The short-cycle, trading-heavy names printed strongly; the two most "compounder-like" peers printed low organic growth on hard comparisons. The Q3 base for Indutrade (organic -1%, margin 14.6%) is easy, which places it closer to the first group.
  3. Three inconsistencies in the AlphaSense reports that the Consumer should not inherit. (a) The guidance-and-consensus report (T-I1) asserts in one paragraph that "the Riksbank and ECB have entered an aggressive rate-cutting cycle in late 2026"; every other source in the files (Nordea, Jefferies, Deutsche Bank, CaixaBank, Morgan Stanley, the macro report OWN-3) has the Riksbank at 1.75% leaning toward hikes to 2.25 to 2.50% by early-to-mid 2027 and the ECB at 2.50% going to 2.75 to 3.00%. I disregard the rate-cutting sentence. (b) The same report gives the Q3 consensus EBITA margin as 15.2% and DNB Carnegie's as 15.1%, while quoting DNB's "adjusted EBITA margin could fail to reach the projected 13% by 2027" in a bear case; the 13% is a bear-case figure, not a forecast. (c) The acquisition-pricing report (OWN-2) reads the DNB Carnegie peer table as showing Indutrade's initial multiples "aligned" with peers at about 7x; DNB's table itself, quoted in three other reports, puts Indutrade's 2023 to 2025 median at 8.6x against 6.6 to 6.8x for Addtech, Lagercrantz and Lifco, and Redeye separately puts Indutrade's initial median at 7x and 9x including maximum earn-outs. I carry the DNB figure as the cohort comparison and the Redeye split as the explanation (the premium is mostly earn-out structure and deal size, partly price).
  4. Two corrections to the second set that the Q3 balance sheet forces. First, the second set's P-02 narrative put year-end 2026 net debt at "about SEK 8.3bn" and leverage at 1.35x; the Financial Analyst's model, run from the same inputs, gives SEK 9,719m, and the company's own figure at 30 September is SEK 9,574m before the October deals. The model is right and the narrative was an arithmetic slip (free cash flow of SEK 2.9bn does not fund SEK 2.3bn of acquisitions plus SEK 1.13bn of dividends). Second, net financial items of SEK -331m for nine months, with net debt higher and policy rates rising, cannot produce the second set's FY2026 input of SEK -410m; the run-rate is about -445m. I change that input (section 6 file) and the acquisitions line, for reasons given in D-01 and D-04.
  5. Bases. As in the second set: EBITA on the company's basis; "adjusted EPS" adds back acquired-intangible amortisation in full (FY2025 SEK 8.83; the model's basis); "reported EPS" is basic EPS after it (FY2025 SEK 7.03). The FactSet consensus EPS blends the two and is not used as a series.

The third set: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Risks

C527 questions
D-0164D-0262D-0350D-0447D-0554D-0658D-0745

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The forecasts for the next 18 months are predictable in their mechanism and only moderately predictable in their outcome. The earlier sets are congruent with each other and with the Q3 balance-sheet lines that have reached the files; the one incongruence that matters is between the research and the model template's guideline fair multiple, and the Consumer should resolve it in the research's favour (a fair multiple of 22 to 27x over 18 months, not 32.7x, and a base-case return of about +25 to +50% rather than +79%). The expected change rests about four-fifths on strengths the company has demonstrated in three cycles and once in this one, and one-fifth on a second quarter of cost discipline (tested today), a sustained organic rate at the decade norm (tested from July 2027) and a small, late recovery in the two weak areas (not yet evidenced). The drivers of the difference are two correlated mechanisms (the deal engine and operating leverage) running through one organisational layer and one macro variable, inside a structure whose level of earnings is among the most diversified in the cohort; the cone on annual group earnings is plus or minus 4% for 2026 and -11% to +9% for 2027, with a negative skew concentrated in the four quarters from Q2 2027 and a positive skew beyond 2028; the cone on the share price is twice as wide because the multiple's level is set by yields that have turned against the cohort, while the discount inside that level is the company's to earn and will take longer than 18 months to close. The disclosure lets the Consumer attribute each quarter's variance to its link in the chain and track the deal engine in real time, and it does not give the price-volume split, the cost base by area, the price paid per deal or anything on control, which are the four places a surprise would come from. The net is a case that can be modelled with confidence at the annual group level, must be judged quarter by quarter, and whose 18-month return depends more on the cohort's multiple than on the company's forecast; the corrections I make to the inputs (a higher interest line from the nine-month actual, a higher acquisitions cash line from the 1.2x-sales rule the company just confirmed) lower EPS by about 1% and raise net debt by about SEK 0.4bn a year without changing the shape.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets?

C64

Question asked How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

Judgement. Highly congruent in substance and in direction, with one structural incongruence that sits not between the two sets but between both sets and the model template they feed, and four smaller jars that the Q3 balance sheet and the new reports now resolve. The two sets describe the same company from two angles and land in the same place: a second-rank Nordic compounder whose two engines have restarted after a two-year stall, whose margin floor and gross margin are demonstrated, whose growth target is aspirational, whose returns are below peers because it pays more per deal, whose balance sheet is not a constraint, and whose share price is set more by the cohort's yield-driven multiple than by its own delivery. Every group score in the first set sits between 48 and 60 and the second set's group score is 57; no group judgement in either set contradicts another. The forecasts in the second set are the first set's qualitative conclusions converted into rules (one-third of a margin point per point of organic growth above 2%; 6 to 7% acquired sales a year at 16 to 18% margins for about 1.2x sales; no dilution, no buyback, progressive dividend), and the first set's three warnings (organic relapse, price paid per deal, control) are the second set's three bear-case triggers. The congruence is a strength for predictability because it means the forecast rests on a single coherent picture rather than on a compromise between views; the incongruence with the template is the thing the Consumer must handle explicitly.

The structural incongruence: the model's guideline fair multiple against both sets' valuation findings. The one-page summary applies a guideline fair multiple of 32.67x two-year-forward earnings at the 18-month column, producing a total shareholder return of +78.7% of which 46.7 points come from the multiple. That multiple is the ten-year 90th-percentile band the dashboard labels "mania" (32.70x); the stock has traded there only at the 2021 peak. The first set's C-83 derived a "defendable" multiple of 27.3x (the 75th percentile), a median of 22.4x and a fair-trough of 17 to 18x, and concluded that the skew from today's 22.9x is roughly symmetric on the multiple alone and negative once the earnings cone is added; its C-82 and C-84 said the sector de-rates together when yields rise and that Indutrade is a "mature engine that needs the re-rating" in a regime that refuses re-ratings; the second set built no multiple view at all. D-07 below finds the level of the cohort's multiple is yield-driven and that the 20% discount to peers is company-driven and will not close within 18 months without a margin and return profile the company has declined to pursue. So the earlier sets, read together, say the fair multiple over 18 months is nearer the median (22.4x) than the mania band, and that the 18-month return is the earnings growth plus the dividend with little or nothing from the multiple: on the model's own earnings, about +25% at 22.4x, +52% at 27.3x, +12% at 20.0x, against +79% at 32.67x (the arithmetic is in D-07). This is not a contradiction inside the research; it is the template's phase assumption (a stock in a peak-delivery phase) disagreeing with the research's phase finding (a stock turning up inside a cohort that is de-rating). The Consumer should carry the research's finding into topics #11 and #12 and treat the guideline figure as the template's upper band.

The jars, and how each resolves.

  1. Cash conversion. The first set read FactSet's after-dividend free-cash-flow line as pre-dividend and concluded conversion would fall to 80 to 90% of net income; the second set corrected it to 95 to 110% after leases. The working-capital report (OWN-4) now supplies the broker definitions that reconcile the two readings: Redeye's lease-adjusted free cash flow to EBITA median of 69% (2014 to 2024) and DNB Carnegie's 63% (2005 to 2023) are measured against EBITA, not net income, and are consistent with roughly 100% of reported net income. The first set's "false friend" hypothesis (inventory releases flattering 2024 to 2025 cash) is also partly right: the company's own "cash conversion above 130%" is operating cash flow over EBITA before leases and capex and did benefit from the 5% inventory cut in 2025. Resolved in the second set's favour, with the caveat that the Q3 operating cash flow of SEK 1,216m (a 38% rise on Q2's SEK 882m, and enough to reduce net debt in a quarter that included the Controlin acquisition) says the recovery has not yet absorbed cash, which is better than either set assumed for H2 2026.
  2. The interest line. The first set expected falling interest to add about a point to EPS growth; the second set, writing after the October regime change, modelled a rising absolute line. The nine-month actual (-331m against -334m) shows the line flat so far, which means the fall the first set expected was already spent by mid-2026 and the rise the second set expected has not yet begun. Both sets' directions were right for their dates; the second set's level was too low (see D-04 and the input change).
  3. The krona. The first set carried a 2 to 3% translation drag on the consensus path; the second set switched to neutral at EUR/SEK 11.20. The macro report confirms the switch (11.11 on 22 October; Lifco, Addtech, Beijer Ref and OEM all reported positive translation in Q3) and adds the next turn: the bank forecasts (Morgan Stanley, Nordea, Deutsche Bank) expect krona recovery through 2027 as rate differentials compress, which would make translation a headwind again in H2 2027. The two sets are congruent once dated; the forecast's "zero translation" is right for the next two to three quarters and optimistic for the following three.
  4. The deal engine's size. The first set said 16 to 20 deals and SEK 2.0 to 2.5bn of acquired sales a year; the second set took SEK 2.0 to 2.2bn for 2026. The actual is 13 deals and SEK 2.08bn of sales by 23 October with two months left, so the sales figure is in range and the count is below it: deals are larger (average SEK 160m against a historical median of SEK 75m). That is congruent with the first set's C-16 and C-18 observation that average size is rising and with its warning that size raises the stakes per deal. What neither set priced correctly is the cash: the preliminary purchase price of SEK 2,508m for nine months (1.22x sales, matching the long-run median) plus SEK 228m of earn-out settlements for earlier years means 2026 cash on acquisitions runs to about SEK 2.6 to 2.8bn, not the SEK 2.3bn the second set entered, and the same 1.2x rule applied to the second set's own acquired-sales path means every later year's cash line is low by about SEK 250 to 300m. I correct this in the inputs.
  5. Internal to the second set. The narrative year-end 2026 net debt (SEK 8.3bn, 1.35x) against the model's SEK 9.7bn (about 1.55x) and the actual SEK 9.6bn at September. The model is right; the narrative's leverage and interest conclusions were drawn from the wrong balance. The investment conclusion does not change (leverage still falls on the forecast, from about 1.55x to about 1.25x by 2030 on my corrected inputs), but the Consumer should quote the model's path, not the second set's text.

Where the two sets jar in emphasis rather than in fact. The first set's management group concluded "the probability of a positive surprise from management action is low"; the second set's P-02 and P-05 identified the unused SEK 6bn of balance-sheet capacity as the upside case. Both are true: the capacity exists and this management has not used it. The first set's narrative group said the share price will be driven by delivery and the cohort multiple, not by narrative events; the second set's P-06 gave each quarterly print a 5 to 15% share-price magnitude. These are the same statement in two registers. I find no place where a reader following one set would be misled by the other once the five jars above are corrected.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and how far does the thesis rely on strengths the business demonstrably has rather than strengths it would need to acquire?

C62

Question asked To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

Judgement. About four-fifths of the forecast difference between the next 18 months to four years and the last two years rests on strengths the company has demonstrated across more than one cycle and that peers in the same position have demonstrated; about one-fifth rests on strengths it has shown once (one quarter, or one half-year) and must repeat; and a small residual (perhaps 30 to 50 basis points of the 2027 to 2028 margin path, and nothing in 2026) rests on capabilities not yet shown. The expected change is therefore plausible as a return to a demonstrated algorithm, and implausible only where the forecast reaches beyond it, which the second set's forecast largely does not. The plausibility is highest for the margin floor, the gross margin and the mechanical acquisition arithmetic, moderate for the deal engine at its new scale and for organic growth at the decade norm, and low for the recovery of the two weak business areas, the 20% return-on-capital target (which the forecast does not require) and the control scaffolding (which the forecast assumes holds without evidence).

Strength by strength.

  • Pricing and the gross margin (demonstrated, high confidence). 34.6% to 35.6% on the company's basis through a seven-quarter volume decline and an input-cost cycle; the same conduct at Addtech (+330 basis points 2022 to 2025), Bufab (twelve consecutive quarters to 33.2%), Lagercrantz (above 39%) and Bossard. The T-D1 report rates this high confidence and I agree. The forecast asks only that it hold flat; the one acquired strength would be holding it in Swedish trading subsidiaries as the krona weakens, where the 2014 to 2016 precedent says the lag to reprice is two to four quarters and the group effect is 10 to 20 basis points. Demonstrated.
  • The 14% EBITA floor (demonstrated). Never below 13.3% in any quarter including the quarter with the write-down; 14.6% average 2021 to 2025. The forecast's bear case (14.0% in 2027) sits on the floor. Demonstrated.
  • Operating leverage on the pre-built cost base (demonstrated in three cycles, repeated once in this one). Q4 2011 (sales +22%, EBITA +54%), 2021 to 2022 (organic +8% and +12%, margin to a then-record 14.7% and 15.2%), Q2 2026 (organic +5%, EBITA +12%); Addtech, Lifco and Bufab showed the same incremental margins in 2026; Bernstein's indicator puts the European short-cycle upcycle six quarters into an average fourteen. The forecast's rule (one-third of a margin point per point of organic growth above 2%) is calibrated on this record and asks for less in 2027 to 2030 than the record delivered in 2011 or 2021. What needs repeating is the cost discipline underneath: the 2024 to Q1 2026 record shows SG&A can grow into a recovery, and the Q1 2026 miss was exactly that. One quarter of evidence that it does not; the Q3 print is the second test. Demonstrated once in this cycle, demonstrated in prior cycles.
  • The acquisition engine at 6 to 7% of sales (demonstrated in H1 and nine months 2026; at the 20 to 30 deal pace, not demonstrated). Thirteen deals and SEK 2.08bn of sales by October against SEK 1.3bn in all of 2025 and SEK 1.6bn in 2024; acquired EBITA contribution back to 7 to 8%; 75% internally sourced; the Addtech precedent that subdividing (2021, and to fifteen subdivision heads in 2025) raised both engines. The T-D1 report's caution is right that H1 2026 could be a release of deals deferred from 2024 to 2025 and that the structure is only proven when it delivers twenty-plus deals through a full cycle. The second set's forecast needs 16 to 20 deals rising to 22 to 25, which is a count the company has not sustained (its ten-year norm is 13 to 16 at smaller size); but it needs SEK 2.3 to 3.0bn of acquired sales, which at the 2026 average size of SEK 160m is 15 to 19 deals, inside the demonstrated range. The forecast therefore rests on the demonstrated engine so long as average deal size stays at 2026's level; the "20 to 30 deals" ambition is not required.
  • Price paid (demonstrated discipline on the company's own measure, not on the cohort's). The nine-month 2026 aggregate of 1.22x sales equals the long-run median, so the step-up in volume has not come with a step-up in price on the EV/sales measure; on EV/EBITA, DNB Carnegie's 8.6x median against 6.6 to 6.8x at peers stands, and Redeye's 7x initial to 9x with maximum earn-outs explains most of it as structure and size rather than auction losses. The forecast assumes 8 to 8.5x all-in and 16 to 18% acquired margins, which is what the company has done; it would need to acquire a strength (lower multiples) only to reach the 20% ROCE target, which the forecast does not assume. Lagercrantz's chief financial officer and Salix's 6.7x record say the sub-EUR-20m market has not repriced in 2026; the T-D1 report rates this medium confidence and so do I.
  • Organic growth of 3 to 4.5% (demonstrated as a decade norm; not yet demonstrated as sustained in this cycle). The decade average is about 4%; the second set asks 4.5% in H2 2026 against -1% and 0% comparisons, 3.5% in 2027, 3% after. The strength to be demonstrated is breadth beyond Life Science and energy into the short-cycle two-thirds, which Q2 2026 showed for one quarter (four of five areas positive, more than half the companies growing). The peer prints of the last ten days (OEM +11%, Bufab +6.5%, Beijer +8%) say short-cycle demand has broadened in Q3; Addtech's +1% and Lifco's +1.6% say the compounder cohort's own organic growth is uneven. Plausible; one quarter short of demonstrated.
  • Recovery of Infrastructure & Construction and Technology & Systems Solutions (not demonstrated). The forecast has I&C from 12.2% to 13.0% by 2028 and T&SS from 13.7% to 15.0%, worth 20 to 30 basis points of group margin over 2027 to 2028. The precedents (Lagercrantz TecSec, Bergman & Beving, Alimak, Volati) say construction-linked units recover from early 2027 at best and with revenue-recognition lags; the UK project companies have a new head but no reported improvement; DNB Carnegie found T&SS the largest deviation in Q1 2026. The T-D1 report rates this low confidence. This is the part of the forecast that rests on a strength to be acquired, and it is small and late.
  • Return on capital employed of 20% (not demonstrated since 2022; not required). DNB Carnegie has 16% in 2027 and 17.4% in 2028 on its definition; the company's own was 18% at June. The forecast's EPS path does not depend on it; the multiple discount partly does (D-07).
  • Control (not demonstrated; assumed). No internal audit function; self-assessment questionnaires; the SEK 125m concealment ran for several quarters. The forecast sets write-downs to zero, which is the base rate for most years and not for all; the first set sized the recurrence at SEK 100 to 300m in a quarter with "plausible" probability. The forecast assumes a strength (detection) the company has said it will not build.
  • The finance handover (demonstrated once). The 2018 Öhman-to-Johnson handover coincided with a reorganisation and produced no disruption; the interim is the former incumbent; the incoming chief financial officer has M&A credentials. Plausible; the risk is a quarter or two of disclosure noise, not a capability gap.

The precedents of relighting both engines after a two-year stall. The files contain one clean success at scale (Addtech: eleven flat organic quarters with EBITA growth throughout, then a reorganisation and a plan for 15 to 20 deals a year; margin expanded for a fifth consecutive year), one partial (Lifco: organic 1.2% in Q1 2026, 4.7% in Q2, 1.6% in Q3; margin held at 23%; acquisitions carried growth), one trading-heavy success (Bufab: twelve quarters of gross-margin expansion then organic growth returning in 2026 at a record margin), and three failures that all involved leverage or integration Indutrade does not have (Storskogen at 2.3x after restructuring; Volati at 4.8x including preference shares with M&A capacity halted; Bergman & Beving's forced cost-cutting). Indutrade's own precedents are 2010 to 2011 (sales -7.5% in 2009, then organic +12% and a record margin by Q4 2011) and 2021 to 2022. The pattern that distinguishes success is unlevered balance sheets, hands-off ownership and a trading or niche-product mix that holds gross margin, all of which Indutrade has; the pattern that distinguishes speed is organic breadth, which it has shown once. Time to full re-engagement in the successes was four to six quarters from the first positive organic quarter, which puts Indutrade's at Q2 to Q4 2027, consistent with the second set's 15.0% margin in 2027.

What this means for the forecast's plausibility by year. 2026: almost entirely demonstrated strengths (easy comparisons, completed deals, a cost base already built, a gross margin already at the level); the residual risk is the Q3 and Q4 prints repeating Q2's cost discipline. 2027: demonstrated strengths for about 80% of the EBITA growth (acquisitions already closed plus those the 2026 run-rate implies, leverage at the decade-norm organic rate), with the 20% residual in sustained organic breadth and the first 10 to 20 basis points from the weak areas. 2028 to 2030: the algorithm at the historical pace, demonstrated over 2005 to 2023, with the only acquired strength being the T&SS and I&C margins (30 to 50 basis points) and the deal count scaling toward 20 at the larger size. On the T-D1 report's own classification, the plausibility is high for leverage and gross margin, medium for deal capacity and multiples, low for the weak areas, and medium for targets and handover; weighting these by their contribution to the forecast gives a plausibility well above neutral and short of high.

D-03

How diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences between the forecasts and the last four to six years' financials?

C50

Question asked In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

Judgement. Concentrated at the level of mechanism, diffuse at the level of source, and correlated at the level of cause. Two mechanisms (the acquired engine and operating leverage) generate about three-quarters of the difference between the forecast and the last two years, and both run through the same organisational layer (35 segment leaders who both source deals and hold costs) and the same macro variable (European short-cycle volume under a rising-rate regime); that is the concentration. Beneath them the sources are as diffuse as any company in the cohort (220 companies, five areas, 35 segments, 30 countries, no customer above 3%, the ten largest subsidiaries 19% of sales); that is the diffusion. The two mechanisms are not independent: the volume that produces leverage is also the volume that makes sellers willing and keeps acquired companies on their earn-out plans, so a relapse would hit both at once, which is why the bear case in D-04 is wider than a two-driver model with independent drivers would give. Against the last four to six years rather than the last two, the picture is different and more comfortable: the forecast's compound EBITA growth (about 11%) equals the 2020 to 2025 rate (11.3%), so the "difference" is mostly a return to a pattern, and the concentration is in what interrupted the pattern (the deal engine stalling and SG&A growing into a volume decline) rather than in anything new.

The arithmetic of the difference, 2025 underlying to 2027 forecast. EBITA rises from SEK 4,575m (14.1% underlying on 2025 sales) to SEK 5,729m (15.0%) in the forecast, a gain of about SEK 1,150m. Attribution on the second set's own rules: acquired companies closed in 2026 and assumed for 2027 at 6.5% of sales and 16 to 18% margins, about SEK 620m (54%); operating leverage from the SG&A ratio falling as organic volume returns, about SEK 300m (26%, the 80 basis points of margin the rule gives for organic growth of 4.5% then 3.5%); organic volume at a constant margin, about SEK 230m (20%); mix and the two weak areas' recovery, about SEK 60 to 100m (5 to 8%), with the first two years' currency at zero. On the reported-EPS line the concentration is slightly higher because the below-EBITA items (amortisation growing with deals, the interest line) are themselves functions of the deal engine. So about 80% of the two-year EBITA difference rests on two mechanisms and one capability. Across the full four years to 2030 the acquired share rises toward 60% as leverage fades, which is the historical norm (DNB Carnegie: acquisitions were more than half of earnings growth historically; the sector digest: unannounced deals are typically two-thirds of a top compounder's growth).

Why the diffusion of sources does not dilute the concentration of mechanism. The D2 report concludes the drivers are "relatively diffuse across 35 segments and 220 subsidiaries, but shared exposures create correlated downside"; that is right and incomplete. The diffusion protects the level of earnings (the 14% floor held through the stall; no subsidiary failure moves the group by more than 1%; the 2009 fall was 7.5% of sales), but the growth of earnings above that level depends on a small number of throughput variables: the deal count and size, the organic rate in the short-cycle two-thirds, and the SG&A ratio. In 2024 to 2025 those three turned together and EBITA was flat for two years despite the diffusion of sources; in H1 2026 they turned back together. The forecast's difference is therefore as concentrated as those three variables, and the law of large numbers that makes the level predictable does not apply to them.

The correlated cause, and what it does to the concentration. The deal engine and operating leverage share two upstream drivers. Organisationally, both are delivered by the segment leaders, who carry 6 to 8 companies each against a capacity of 10 to 15 and are tasked with both coaching (cost and margin) and sourcing (one deal a year); a layer that is stretched by a rising deal count has less capacity for the monthly scorecards that hold costs, which is the mechanism by which the T-D1 report warns of "organisational indigestion". Macroeconomically, both depend on European short-cycle volume under the rates regime: volume produces leverage directly, and it produces deals indirectly because sellers wait for better times in downturns (Redeye's historical finding) and because earn-out structures are only cheap when acquired companies hit their plans (a positive remeasurement, as in 2025's SEK 208m, means they did not). The 2024 to 2025 stall is the demonstration: organic growth went to -2%, SG&A ratio rose 200 basis points, deal contribution halved, and the three moved together. For the third set's purpose this means the effective number of independent drivers behind the forecast difference is closer to two than to four, and the bear case must be built by moving them together, which the second set's bear case (organic 0%, SG&A growing, fifteen deals, one control failure) correctly does.

What would make the difference fail, in order of damage. (1) Organic growth below 2% in 2027 (the break-even rate in the leverage rule): removes the SEK 300m of leverage and part of the volume contribution, and on the correlation above probably takes the deal count down with it; the D2 report notes that two-thirds of sales are cyclical and half early-cyclical. (2) The deal count falling below 15 at the 2026 average size, or average size falling back toward SEK 100m: removes 1 to 2 points of annual EBITA growth. (3) SG&A growing faster than sales for two consecutive quarters of positive organic growth: converts the leverage into its 2024 to 2025 mirror. (4) A control failure of the UK kind: episodic, SEK 100 to 300m in a quarter. (5) Currency: SEK 18m of net profit per 1%, both ways. The first three are one variable in three forms; the fourth and fifth are independent of it. Against this, two things would make the difference larger than forecast and are also concentrated: the balance sheet's spare SEK 6bn deployed at a faster deal pace (management's choice, not the market's), and organic growth of 5 to 6% sustained through 2027 (the market's, not management's).

For the Consumer. The predictability of the forecast is the predictability of three throughput variables that move together, inside a structure that makes the level of earnings very predictable. That is a better configuration than a single-product or single-customer concentration (nothing here is fragile at the level of a contract or a licence), and a worse one than a diversified industrial whose earnings drivers are independent (the three variables share a cause). The honest description is "two correlated mechanisms, many independent sources", and the score reflects that the concentration is in mechanisms the company controls partly (deals, costs) and in a macro variable it does not control at all.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

❌ D47

Judgement. The cone on earnings is moderately narrow for 2026 and moderately wide for 2027; the cone on the share price is wide in both years because the multiple is the larger variable. On the model's adjusted basis, I put FY2026 EPS within plus or minus 4% of SEK 10.20 (my corrected base) with about 70% probability, and FY2027 EPS between SEK 10.3 (bear) and SEK 12.6 (bull) around a base of SEK 11.55, which is -11% to +9% with about 70% probability inside and the tails outside. EBITA: 2026 SEK 5.0 to 5.15bn around 5.08bn; 2027 SEK 5.1 to 6.2bn around 5.73bn. The skew is negative on earnings over the twelve months from the Q2 2027 print and positive beyond 2028; it is close to symmetric on the multiple from today's 22.9x two-year-forward P/E, and negative on the combination because the earnings bear case and the multiple bear case share a cause (the rates regime). Predictability of the forecasts over 18 months is therefore moderate at the annual group level, low at the quarterly level, and low for the share price; in the instruction's terms, the case is predictable enough to be modelled quarter by quarter and not predictable enough to be bought on the forecast alone.

The width, built from the pieces.

  • Organic growth. The company's own decade range by quarter is -4% to +13%; the cohort's current prints run from +1% (Addtech, Lifco +1.6%) to +11% (OEM, Sdiptech). For H2 2026 against -1% and 0% bases, the plausible range is +2% to +7% (base 4.5%); for 2027 against +5% from Q2, 0% to +5.5% (base 3.5%). Each point of organic growth is about 0.33 points of margin and about 2.1x in EBITA growth at the base, so the 2027 organic range alone is worth about SEK 400m of EBITA either way (7%).
  • The acquired engine. Thirteen deals and SEK 2.08bn of sales are closed for 2026; 2027's contribution from deals already closed is largely known (full-year effects of 2026 deals, about 4% of sales), so the 2027 range from deals is narrow (plus or minus 1.5 points of sales growth depending on the 2027 count), and widens from 2028.
  • Costs. The SG&A ratio: 22.5% of sales in 2025; the forecast has it drifting toward 21.6% by 2028; the plausible range for 2027 is 21.8% to 22.8%, worth about SEK 380m of EBITA (7%) either way, and it is correlated with the organic range (costs are cut with a lag on the way down).
  • Unforecastable items. Earn-out remeasurements netted +SEK 208m in 2025 and -14m in Q2 2026; write-downs SEK 125m in Q4 2025; the D2 report counts Q4 2025 (-14% against consensus), Q1 2026 (-5%) and Q2 2026 (+11 to 12%) as the last three quarterly outcomes against forecasts, and notes that dispersion widens, not narrows, in the first year of a recovery because the areas diverge. I carry plus or minus 8% of a quarter's EBITA and plus or minus 3% of a year's as the noise band.
  • Currency. Zero in the base; SEK 18m of net profit per 1%; the bank forecasts' krona recovery in 2027 is worth 2 to 3% of 2027 net profit if it arrives (EUR/SEK back toward 10.8 to 10.9); the opposite if the risk-off regime weakens the krona further.
  • The interest line. The corrected base is -445m (2026) and -485m (2027); the range on the policy path (Riksbank 2.00 to 2.50%, ECB 2.50 to 3.00% by mid-2027) is about plus or minus SEK 30m, 0.7% of pre-tax profit; small.

Adding these with their correlations: FY2026 adjusted EPS SEK 9.8 to 10.6 (the year is three-quarters done once the Q3 print is read, the comparisons are easy and the deals are closed); FY2027 SEK 10.3 to 12.6 as above. The 2027 bear case (organic 0%, SG&A growing into the recovery, fifteen deals, one SEK 100 to 200m control failure) gives EBITA of about SEK 5.1bn and a 14.0% margin, flat on 2026; it does not break the floor, the dividend or the deal programme, and it does break the re-rating. The bull case (organic 5 to 6% through 2027 as the PMI lead plays out and construction turns early, twenty-plus deals at 7.5x, the krona weak) gives SEK 6.2bn and 15.6%.

The skew, by horizon. Over the next two quarters (Q3 and Q4 2026) the skew is mildly positive: easy comparisons, closed deals, the three October acquisitions adding about 1.3% to 2027 sales already, neutral-to-positive translation, the peers' short-cycle prints, and the Q3 cash flow and net-debt lines that reached the files (operating cash flow SEK 1,216m, net debt down sequentially) all pointing to a quarter in which cash, at least, was strong. Over the four quarters from Q2 2027 the skew is negative: the hard comparison (+5% organic, 14.7%), the rates regime (Sweden among the most rate-sensitive economies; the DNB strategy note of 13 October counts profit warnings broadening among Swedish mid-caps), the bank forecasts of krona recovery, the first-year-of-recovery dispersion finding, and the cost structure's inability to offset a volume relapse quickly. Beyond 2028 the skew is positive because the consensus and the forecast both exclude deals not yet announced, and the balance sheet can fund 50% more than the base case. The net over the 18 months to April 2028 is a small negative skew on earnings, which the second set also found.

The multiple's cone, separately. Today's two-year-forward P/E of 22.9x sits at the 29th percentile of five years (range 17.9x to 38.2x, median 25.1x) and the 55th percentile of ten years on the dashboard's current P/E. The historical precedents in the files for a rising-yield regime are severe: the Redeye serial-acquirer index's median EV/EBITDA fell from 30x to 15x in 2021 to 2022, DNB Carnegie recorded 20 to 30% multiple contractions for prominent compounders in early 2022, Lagercrantz fell 20% in a month on double-digit earnings growth, Indutrade itself fell from SEK 290 (split-adjusted) to 170 in 2021 to 2022 "despite stable fundamentals", and OEM International's multiple went from 25x to 18x in four months in 2026 on fund outflows. The precedents for the other direction are the 45% rebound after Q2 2026 on one quarter's delivery and the 2024 peak at 27x. A plausible 18-month range for the two-year-forward P/E is 18x to 27x, roughly plus or minus 20% around today, which on flat earnings is a share-price range of about SEK 215 to 320, wider than the whole earnings cone. The two cones are correlated through rates: a Swedish rates shock that produces the earnings bear case also produces the multiple bear case, so the joint distribution has a fatter lower tail than either alone.

Predictability, stated plainly. The group-level annual forecast is reliable within about plus or minus 1.5% on revenue and plus or minus 40 basis points on the EBITA margin for 2026, and within plus or minus 3% and plus or minus 80 basis points for 2027, with 60 to 70% probability; the quarterly figures are not reliable within less than plus or minus 8% of EBITA; the share price is not predictable from the earnings over 18 months because the multiple's variance is twice the earnings' variance and correlated with it. That is a case whose earnings can be forecast and whose return cannot; the score records the earnings side, which is what the question asks, discounted for the correlation that makes the downside tail fatter than the upside.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and the key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

C54

Question asked How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

Judgement. Helpful on the two drivers that matter most (organic volume and the deal engine) and on the margin floor, unhelpful on the three things that would warn earliest (price against volume, the cost base by area, and control), and silent between quarters on everything except acquisitions. The six months to late April 2027 contain three dated disclosures (Q3 today, Q4 on 27 January 2027 with the dividend proposal and the full-year deal count, Q1 2027 in April under the new chief financial officer) plus a stream of acquisition press releases that is the only real-time signal the company gives and that, unusually, carries the acquired company's sales and sometimes its margin. For a Consumer who must judge the forecast quarter by quarter, the disclosure is adequate for confirmation after the fact and poor for anticipation; the leading indicators have to come from outside (PMIs, peers' prints, rates, the krona).

What is disclosed, and how it maps to the forecast's drivers.

  • Organic growth and orders: group and five-area organic sales growth, order intake, book-to-bill and a qualitative backlog statement each quarter, plus the share of subsidiaries growing. This is the forecast's largest variable and the disclosure is direct, though lagged a quarter and without a price-volume split (so a 4% organic print cannot be read as 2% price plus 2% volume or 4% price and no volume, which matters for working capital and for what the PMI says next).
  • The deal engine: every deal announced on signing with annual sales, business area and often margin; the quarterly report gives the acquired contribution to sales and EBITA, the preliminary purchase price in aggregate, the contingent consideration recognised and its maximum. This is the best-disclosed driver in the cohort and lets the Consumer run the 1.2x-sales rule in real time. Not disclosed: the multiple per deal, the acquired company's first-year performance, the earn-out conditions.
  • The margin and its bridge: EBITA and margin by area, the gross margin, and an "underlying" margin where the company strips remeasurements and write-downs; selling and administrative costs are in the income statement so the SG&A ratio is derivable each quarter; the currency bridge (organic, acquired, currency) is given. Not disclosed: SG&A by area, headcount by area, or any statement of what the cost base is built for.
  • Below the line: net financial items quarterly (the nine-month figure of SEK -331m reached the files), the tax rate, the amortisation of acquired intangibles, the contingent consideration roll-forward, lease repayments, and the company's cash conversion on its own definition (operating cash flow over EBITA). Adequate for the interest and amortisation lines; the Consumer must convert the company's cash conversion to a net-income basis each time.
  • Control: nothing between events. The corporate governance report describes self-assessment questionnaires and selective validation; there is no internal audit, no disclosure of the number of subsidiaries reviewed, and the first sign of a problem of the UK kind is the write-down itself.

What the next six months will and will not reveal. Today's Q3 print answers the question that matters most for 2026 (does the Q2 cost discipline repeat: the SG&A ratio against 22.5% and the margin against 14.6%); the 27 January print answers the deal count for 2026 (fifteen to sixteen on the current pace against Redeye's eighteen), the underlying Q4 margin against 14.9%, the dividend (SEK 3.30 to 3.40) and whether the backlog statement for 2027 still says "extends through" the following quarters; the April print is the first under Anna Vilogorac and the first where disclosure practice could change (the T-D1 report notes her "long-term financial integration playbook remains unstated"; a chief financial officer from Ratos is more likely to add than remove disclosure, but a quarter of definitional change is possible). None of the three will reveal the price-volume split, the per-deal multiple or the state of control, and none gives forward guidance, so the Consumer's monitoring of 2027 (the hard comparison from Q2 2027) rests on the backlog tone and on external indicators until July 2027.

The monitoring plan this disclosure supports, with thresholds. (1) Organic growth: at or above 3% in Q3 and Q4 2026 confirms the base; below 2% in either moves me to the bear path. (2) SG&A growth against sales growth: SG&A below sales in both quarters confirms leverage; above sales in either with positive organic growth is the 2024 pattern. (3) Deal flow: the SEK of acquired annual sales announced per quarter, with SEK 500m a quarter sustaining the forecast; a count below fifteen for 2026 or a quarter with none is a warning. (4) Area margins: T&SS above 14% and I&C above 12% by Q4 2026 would be ahead of the forecast; below 13.5% and 11.5% behind it. (5) Net financial items: about SEK 110 to 120m a quarter is the corrected base; above 130m means rates or debt are running ahead. (6) Contingent consideration remeasurements: a large positive remeasurement is bad news (acquired companies missing plans), and the Consumer should read it the opposite way from the income statement. (7) The currency bridge: zero to +2% expected on sales for Q3 and Q4; a negative figure in 2027 is the krona-recovery path. Outside the company: the weighted Swedish and eurozone PMI (above 50 since mid-2026; a fall below 50 leads organic growth down by two to five quarters), the Riksbank in November and February, and the peers' prints in the same fortnight each quarter.

Against the cohort. Standard for Lifco and Addtech (no guidance, areas disclosed, deals announced), far less than Diploma (guidance raised three times in 2026 with a full bridge) and less than Addtech on working-capital metrics (return on working capital published). The helpfulness is therefore average for the cohort, below average for a guided industrial, and better than average on the one driver (deals) that the consensus most under-models.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into operational drivers, revenue, cost and margin lines, and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

C58

Question asked How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

Judgement. The internal transmission is unusually clear for a decentralised group because the company has published the building blocks of its algorithm and the arithmetic is mechanical; the external transmission is clear for currency and rates, moderately clear for the industrial cycle, and opaque for the two value-chain variables that matter most to returns (the price paid for targets and the realised price charged to customers). Of the three things the company says it will achieve, two trace cleanly (10% sales growth, 14% margin) and one does not trace at all (20% return on capital employed, which no two parties in the files compute the same way). The Consumer can build the chain from targets to EPS line by line and should expect each link to be visible in the quarterly report except the cost base by area and the price-volume split.

The internal chain, link by link.

  1. Sales growth of at least 10% = acquired growth + organic growth + currency. Acquired growth traces to the deal count times average size times 1.2x sales (the company states 5 to 8x EBITA on initial consideration, about 0.4x sales of earn-outs, SEK 30 to 250m target size, one deal per segment per year across 35 segments) and is observable deal by deal; the 2026 actual (13 deals, SEK 2.08bn, 1.22x) traces exactly. Organic growth is not guided and traces to the PMI with a two-to-five-quarter lead, to the backlog statement, and to the area mix (Life Science at 13% organic in Q2, I&C at -3%). Currency traces to the stated sensitivity (SEK 243m of sales per 1%). The link is clear; the organic component is the one the company itself cannot forecast.
  2. EBITA margin of at least 14% = gross margin (flat at 35.5% by stated culture: "protect the gross margin under all circumstances") less selling and administrative costs (about 22.5% of sales in 2025, falling as volume returns at the one-third-of-a-point rule) plus mix (acquired companies at 16 to 18%, Life Science at 18%) plus the area recoveries. Each piece is observable quarterly at group level; the area margins are disclosed; the cost base by area is not. The company's own statement that "costs should not rise proportionately with revenue" is the only forward cost guidance and it traces directly to the leverage rule.
  3. From EBITA to EPS = less amortisation of acquired intangibles (about 40% of consideration over about ten years; SEK 656m in 2025, growing with deals; the whole difference between the two EPS bases), less net financial items (SEK 61m per percentage point of rates on about SEK 6bn of floating debt; lease interest and earn-out unwind inside the line), at a 23 to 24% tax rate drifting up with the jurisdiction mix, over a fixed 364.3m shares. Every link is disclosed and the sensitivities are published; the only judgement is which EPS basis the market prices on, and the files show the sell-side split three to three.
  4. Return on capital employed of at least 20%: does not trace. The company reports 18% at June 2026; DNB Carnegie computes 13.8% for 2026 rising to 17.4% in 2028 on a definition that includes accumulated amortisation; Redeye reports 18% for 2025 with Life Science at 26%. The target is a function of the entry multiple (8 to 8.5x all-in), the acquired margin and the three-to-five-year improvement to the group average, none of which is disclosed per deal, so the Consumer cannot check progress toward it between annual reports and the sell-side uses its own version to justify the discount. This is the one target whose transmission to the share price is clearer than its transmission from the operations.
  5. Dividend and share count: 30 to 50% payout, dividends rising in absolute terms each year (chief executive), no buybacks, hedged incentive programmes; traces exactly.

The external chain.

  • Industrial cycle to organic growth: the weighted PMI crossed 53 in June 2026 and has led organic acceleration by two to five quarters historically; Bernstein's short-cycle indicator and Morgan Stanley's distributor survey (volumes stronger, pricing gap improving) corroborate; the peers' prints are the quarterly cross-check. Clear in direction, imprecise in magnitude, and the company does not split its exposure beyond "half early-cyclical, half late-cyclical" (DNB Carnegie).
  • Rates to EPS and to the multiple: SEK 61m per point to pre-tax profit (1.5%) is clear and small; the transmission to the multiple (the earnings yield against the ten-year euro swap, which DNB Carnegie uses to set target multiples and which tightened in Q3 2026) is clear and large, and is the dominant external transmission to the share price (D-07).
  • Currency to EPS: SEK 18m of net profit per 1%, unhedged by policy, with the bridge disclosed quarterly; the clearest link in the chain, and the one the sell-side updates first (DNB Carnegie's +1% on 9 October was currency).
  • The M&A market to returns: the price paid is not disclosed per deal; the aggregate (1.22x sales) is; the cohort's realised multiples are known only through broker tables that disagree (8.6x against 7x initial). This link is opaque at exactly the point where the return on new capital is decided.
  • Customer bargaining power to the gross margin: no price-volume split, so the margin is observable but not its cause; the October automation-channel expert evidence (suppliers recovering 2 to 3% of 3 to 4% inflation) cannot be checked against the company's numbers until the gross margin moves.
  • Regulation, suppliers, substitutes: slow and diffuse; no quarterly transmission to model.

What this means for predictability. The internal clarity means a forecast error can be located within a quarter of its appearing (organic, cost, deal, below-the-line) and the Consumer can attribute a miss to its link; that raises the usefulness of each print. The external opacity on price paid and price charged means the two variables that would most change the four-year return (acquisition returns, gross-margin durability) are the two the Consumer will see last. The absence of an operating ROCE trace means the sell-side's main justification for the discount is not something the company's disclosure can rebut quarter by quarter. On balance the transmission is clearer than at most decentralised groups and clearer than the cohort average, and the one broken link is the one the valuation argument runs through.

D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (rolling six months), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

❌ D45

Question asked To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

Judgement. Over the last six months the share price has moved mostly on its own: the correlation with the dashboard's peer basket fell from 0.68 to 0.70 through 2024 and 2025 to 0.44 to 0.51 in 2026, the share of daily variance the basket explains fell from 47% to 19 to 26%, and the stock returned +21.3% against the basket's +15.4% with a beta that rose to 1.17 to 1.25. That is the signature of a stock re-priced on company events (the April miss, the July beat, the write-down, the Lifco contagion in January) inside a sector that was itself moving on yields. The multiple decomposes the same way: its level is set by the cohort and the cohort's level is set by yields (the earnings yield against the ten-year euro swap, the 2021 to 2022 halving, the October 2026 pause); its discount to the cohort (15 to 20% on EV/EBITA, DNB Carnegie's explicit 20% target discount) is set by company facts (organic growth, return on capital, price paid, cash conversion on the brokers' definitions) and has been stable for the period. So the multiple is industry-driven in level and company-driven in relative position, and the last six months' price move was the relative position changing (the discount narrowing from the 2025 lows) while the level paused. For predictability over 18 months this is a mixed finding: delivery is paid for (the discount can move), but the larger component of the multiple is outside the company's control, and the two can move against each other as they did in October 2026, when the cohort fell on the swap rate while Indutrade's own delivery expectations rose.

The co-movement table, read with its caveat. The basket in the table excludes the four peers that matter most (Addtech, Beijer Ref, Lagercrantz, OEM International have no price history in it) and is dominated by non-Nordic distributors (Bunzl, Brenntag, IMCD, Azelis, Rexel, RS Group, Bossard) plus Bufab and Diploma. The stock's highest pairwise correlations are with the two most compounder-like names in it, Diploma (0.55, R-squared 30%) and Bufab (0.46, 21%), and its lowest with the chemical distributors (IMCD 0.13, Azelis 0.17); so the measured 0.51 understates the true co-movement with the Swedish compounder cohort, which the OWN-6 report describes as being valued "as a highly unified asset class" during macro moves with Indutrade carrying a "structurally lower beta" because of its diversification. Two conclusions survive the caveat. First, the direction of change is robust: the correlation with every basket member fell through 2026, which only happens when the stock's own news dominates. Second, the beta rising above 1 while the correlation fell means the stock has been more volatile than the basket and less aligned with it, which is what a 45% three-month rebound after a 48% drawdown produces.

What set the price in the episodes, from the files. 2021 to 2022: yields (the serial-acquirer index's median EV/EBITDA from 30x to 15x; the stock from SEK 290 to 170 "despite stable underlying fundamentals"); industry-driven. February 2025 peak at SEK 332.60: cohort multiples at 27x and the stock's own Q4 2024 margin; both. Mid-2025 fall toward SEK 170 to 181: the -4% organic quarter, the margin to 13.7%, the downgrade to Hold, then the UK write-down; company-driven, with the discount to Lagercrantz widening to a record 20%. January 2026: the Lifco short-seller note; cohort-driven (fund flows out of decentralised models). April 2026: the Q1 miss on opex; company. July 2026 onward: the Q2 beat and the 45% rebound; company. Early October 2026: the swap-rate tightening and the sector pause while brokers trimmed targets on yield grounds (DNB Carnegie to SEK 260 from 265 with unchanged estimates); industry. The pattern is alternation, not mixture: in any given month one of the two dominates, and over the last six months the company one has.

Why the discount is company-driven and will not close quickly. DNB Carnegie values Indutrade at 17.4 to 17.5x 2027 EV/EBITA against Addtech 20.3x, Lagercrantz 21.3 to 21.8x and Lifco 19.2x, applying a 20% discount for lower over-the-cycle organic growth (about 4% against high single digits), a 2026 adjusted ROCE of 13.8% against 24% at Addtech, an 8.6x median entry multiple against 6.7x, and cash conversion of 63% on its EBITA-based definition against about 70% at Lifco and Addtech. The OWN-6 report finds "an extremely strong relationship" between ROCE and multiples across the sector and that Lifco's 127% ROCE excluding goodwill drives its premium. Each of those four facts is a company choice or a company outcome, and the first set concluded management has declined to pursue the standards that would change them (no margin-target raise, no pricing programme, no proprietary-share target, no lower entry multiples). Redeye's counter-view (the stock at a 19% discount to its own two- and five-year medians; a 34x historical median P/E implying SEK 342) is a within-company argument about mean reversion, not a cohort argument. Closing a 20% EV/EBITA discount is worth about 25% on the share price and would require two to three years of organic growth and ROCE matching peers; the 18-month evidence will be one year of easy comparisons and one hard one.

The level: yields, and what the files say about 2026. The cohort's level moved with the ten-year euro swap and the US ten-year through 2026 (DNB Carnegie's sector reports of 8 September and 5 October; Deutsche Bank's 2022 finding that Bravida's forward earnings yield tracked the US ten-year; the OWN-3 report's WACC rising from about 8% pre-tax in 2020 to about 10% post-tax in 2025 in the company's own disclosure). The two sides of the 2026 debate in the files are both right in their quarter: the proven-compounder premium over other compounders held at its five-year average of 48% through the summer (DNB Carnegie, 28 August), and the whole cohort paused in early October as the swap rate rose. With the Riksbank and ECB leaning toward hikes and the US ten-year at about 5%, the level's skew over 18 months is down-to-flat; the first set's C-83 arithmetic (fair trough 17 to 18x, about 25% below today) and the 2021 to 2022 precedent bound the downside. Nothing in the company's delivery can move the level; it can only move the discount inside it.

The arithmetic the Consumer needs for the fair multiple and the return (topics #11 and #12). The model's 18-month column applies 32.67x to about SEK 14.3 of two-year-forward adjusted EPS (468.44 divided by 32.67) for a value of SEK 468 and a total return of +78.7%, of which the multiple (from 21.2x today on the model's measure) is 46.7 points. On the same earnings: at the dashboard's "defendable" 27.3x, about SEK 391, a total return of about +52%; at the ten-year median 22.4x, about SEK 321, about +25%; at the "conservative" 20.0x, about SEK 287, about +12%; at the "crash" 15.3x, about SEK 219, about -14%. On my corrected EPS (about 1% lower) each figure is about SEK 3 to 5 lower. The research in both sets says the stock is turning up inside a cohort that is de-rating, not in a peak-delivery phase, which places the fair multiple over 18 months between the median and the defendable band (22 to 27x) and the base-case 18-month return between +25% and +50%, almost all of it earnings growth and dividends; the 32.67x figure requires the yield regime to reverse and the discount to close at once, which is the bull case for both components together and has a probability I would put below one in five.

For predictability. A multiple that is 74% idiosyncratic over six months looks like a company-driven multiple, but the idiosyncratic part is the discount re-pricing on quarterly delivery, which the first set correctly described as 5 to 15% share-price events in either direction; the industry-driven part is larger, slower and currently adverse. That is a configuration in which the forecasts can be right and the share price wrong for a year, which is what the question asks me to score.
